%% toc: P1 8 8.1,8.2,8.5
\chapter{Registry Extracts by Letter Family and Readings of Part 8 Operators}
\chaptermark{Registry Extracts and Readings}
\label{sec:p01-w02b-p01-012-extracts}
\index{symbol registry!extracts}\index{Greek letters}\index{Fraktur letters}\index{blackboard bold}\index{precedence!Part 8 operators}

\emph{Purpose.}  A reader who meets a Greek, Fraktur or blackboard letter,
or a connective, quantifier, turnstile or metavariable of the first two
parts, needs the role that the symbol registry records for it and the
scope inside which that role holds.  A reader who meets an operator of
Part~8 in an expression with omitted parentheses needs its rank and
association, or the rule of its own grammar that fixes the reading.  This
chapter supplies both.  It extracts the relevant rows of
\texttt{registry/symbols.csv} in full, under explicit selection rules, and
it states the rank and association, or the reading fixed by delimiters or
by the grammar, of forty-one Part~8 operators recorded in thirty registry
rows and of Kripke's local-domain binders.

\emph{Inputs.}  The registry, read when these tables were drawn up, with
10080 rows; the letter-family conventions of
\cref{sec:p01-w02-p01-024-letters}; the fixity and association conventions
\cref{def:p01-w02-p01-026-precedence} and
\cref{def:p01-w02-p01-024-fixity}, with Table~\ref{tab:p01-precedence} and
Table~\ref{tab:p01-w02-p01-024-ranks}; and the Part~8 definitions
\cref{def:p08-inq-language,def:p08-inq-support,def:p08-inq-tensor,thm:p08-ltl-strict-bridge,def:p08-pn-language-model,def:p08-bvb501-hybrid,def:p08-bvb501-conditional,def:p08-bvb501-until-check,def:p08-bvb501-until-since,def:p08-bvb501-language,def:p08-bvb501-universal,def:p08-bvb501-pdl,def:p08-bvb501-mu,def:p08-sa-domains,def:p08-sa-concepts,def:p08-sa-operators,def:p08-sa-binding,def:p08-montague-intensional-language,def:p08-montague-predicate-names,def:p08-rat26-epistemic-update,def:p08-rat26-soft-update,def:p08-klm-assertions}.

\emph{Conclusions.}  Tables~\ref{tab:p01-w02b-p01-012-greek},
\ref{tab:p01-w02b-p01-012-fraktur} and~\ref{tab:p01-w02b-p01-012-blackboard}
list, with the recorded role and the recorded scope, every registry row
selected by \cref{def:p01-w02b-p01-012-selection}: 2789 Greek
rows, 116 Fraktur rows and 148 blackboard
rows.  Table~\ref{tab:p01-w02b-p01-012-parts12} lists all 1441
registry rows of global scope, with a scope opening in Part~1 or Part~2, or
with a scope label that names Part~2 but is declared in Part~3, and so
every connective, quantifier, turnstile and metavariable row of
those parts.  \cref{def:p01-w02b-p01-012-p8-readings} gives a rank and an
association, or a reading fixed by delimiters or by the grammar, to each of
the forty-one Part~8 operators recorded in thirty registry rows and to
Kripke's local-domain binders of the \emph{Considerations} comparison, and
says how the other Part~8 rows without a rank combine.  Twenty of these
thirty-one rows record the rank given there and are listed with it in
Table~\ref{tab:p01-precedence}; the other eleven have the precedence entry
\emph{n/a}.
\cref{thm:p01-w02b-p01-012-iterated-support} shows that the chosen
association of the two inquisitive disjunctions never changes which states
support a formula, \cref{thm:p01-w02b-p01-012-program-bracketing} shows the
same for the transition relations of iterated programs,
\cref{thm:p01-w02b-p01-012-strict-reading} derives the readings of the
strict-until bridge, and \cref{thm:p01-w02b-p01-012-epistemic-reading}
those of the announcement law and the greatest-fixed-point identity.  The proofs are in the annex.

\section{Selection rules and row counts}
\label{sec:p01-w02b-p01-012-selection}

\begin{hbconvention}{def:p01-w02b-p01-012-selection}{Which registry rows the extracts contain}
A registry row has seven columns: glyph, macro, role, scope, precedence,
first use and notes.  The extracts select rows by the glyph column and the
scope column only, and print the glyph, the role, the scope and (in the
Parts~1--2 table) the precedence column of each selected row.
\begin{enumerate}
\item A row belongs to the \emph{Greek} family when its glyph contains a
  Greek letter: a command \texttt{\textbackslash alpha} to
  \texttt{\textbackslash omega} or \texttt{\textbackslash Gamma} to
  \texttt{\textbackslash Omega}, with or without the prefix
  \texttt{var}, or a character of the Unicode Greek block, code points
  \texttt{0370} to \texttt{03FF} (hexadecimal).
\item A row belongs to the \emph{Fraktur} family when its glyph contains
  \texttt{\textbackslash mathfrak}.
\item A row belongs to the \emph{blackboard} family when its glyph contains
  \texttt{\textbackslash mathbb} or one of the handbook shorthands
  \texttt{\textbackslash N}, \texttt{\textbackslash Z},
  \texttt{\textbackslash Q}, \texttt{\textbackslash R}, each of which
  typesets a blackboard letter.
\item A row belongs to the \emph{Parts~1--2} extract when its recorded scope
  is \emph{global} or opens in Part~1 or Part~2, and also when its
  recorded scope is a single label whose identifier begins with
  \texttt{p01-} or \texttt{p02-} although the label is declared in a later
  part.
\end{enumerate}
The part in which a scope \emph{opens} is the smallest part number among
the passages of the handbook that the scope denotes: the part named in a
scope such as ``P2 prop-syntax-semantics'', the first part of a range
such as ``Parts~4--5 from decision-hierarchy to arithmetic-coding'', and
the part in which a section, definition, theorem or annex label is
declared.  Rows are grouped by that part, global rows first, and keep
their registry order within a group.  A row selected only by the second
clause of item~4 is grouped by its opening part in the family tables and
in Table~\ref{tab:p01-w02b-p01-012-counts}, and in the Parts~1--2 table it
forms a final group of its own.  The current registry has three such rows:
\(e(\mathfrak A)\), \(E(A)\) and \(\mathsf{NP}_1\), whose scope
\emph{def:p02-w02-p02-036-fagin-encoding} is the encoding convention for
Fagin's theorem.  That convention is cited from the decidable-fragments
chapter of Part~2 and declared in the finite-model-theory chapter of
Part~3, so its scope opens in Part~3.  A row that introduces several
symbols at once is listed whole when one of its symbols qualifies, so that
role and scope read exactly as recorded.  The scope cell reproduces the
recorded scope character for character; only invisible line-break points
are added, and identifiers in which letters and digits are fused are set
in typewriter type.  The role cell reproduces the recorded role, except
for the velocity potential \(\phi\) of the water-model definition of
Part~15, whose role is given in the paraphrase \emph{velocity potential of
the linearized free-surface flow model}.
\end{hbconvention}

A typeface assigns no role by itself.  The role of a letter in a passage is
the role of a row whose scope contains that passage, and two rows that give
one glyph different roles have disjoint scopes unless the registry records
an explicit override.  No Fraktur row has global scope, and the only
blackboard rows of global scope are those of \(\mathbb N\), \(\mathbb Z\),
\(\mathbb Q\) and \(\mathbb R\).

Table~\ref{tab:p01-w02b-p01-012-counts} counts rows, not letters: one row
may carry several letters of a family, and one letter may occur in many
rows.  The Fraktur family has 116 rows; the
29 rows opening in Part~1 and the 29
opening in Part~2 include the formula-schema metavariables
\(\mathfrak s,\mathfrak t\) of the schema and code chapter of Part~1, the
structures \(\mathfrak M,\mathfrak N\) of the Barwise setting of Part~2,
the structures of the monadic decision procedure of Part~2, and the
formula metavariable \(\mathfrak A\) of Gentzen's schemata in the
natural-deduction chapter of Part~2.  Five of the Part~1 rows, for
\(\mathfrak R\), \(\mathfrak D\), \(\mathfrak n\), \(\mathfrak Q\) and
\(\mathfrak Z\), have the scope of this chapter's extracts
(\cref{sec:p01-w02b-p01-012-extracts}): they record that each occurrence
of these letters in the tables of this chapter mentions the glyph of a row
of another scope and gives the letter no role of its own here.  The same
Fraktur letter can carry
unrelated roles in different parts: \(\mathfrak R(\lambda)\) is a resolvent
in the semigroup sections of Part~15, and \(\mathfrak N_n\) is Rabin's
structure of the \(n\)-successor tree in Part~9.  The
Greek family includes the family-member rows that record the propositional
and first-order formula metavariables \(\alpha,\beta,\gamma\) of Part~2
one letter at a time, each with the scope of the passage that uses it; the
term-metavariable members \(r,t,u\) and the formula-metavariable member
\(A\) of the same group are not Greek and appear in
Table~\ref{tab:p01-w02b-p01-012-parts12}.

\begin{hbexample}{def:p01-w02b-p01-012-lookup}{Looking up a letter}
\begin{enumerate}
\item In the Hilbert calculus of Part~2 the letters \(\alpha,\beta,\gamma\)
  are formula metavariables: Table~\ref{tab:p01-w02b-p01-012-greek} has a
  row for each of the three letters with the role \emph{propositional
  formula metavariables symbol family member} and the scope
  \emph{sec:p02-hilbert-cpl}.  In the set-theory chapters of Part~6 the
  letters \(\alpha,\beta\) are read from the row with the role
  \emph{ordinal metavariables} and the scope \emph{P6 zf-zfc to
  determinacy}; neither reading crosses into the other scope.
\item The letter \(\mathfrak s\) in the schema chapter of Part~1 is a
  formula-schema metavariable, by the row with scope
  \emph{sec:p01-w02-p01-004-schemas-codes}; the related row
  \(\lambda;\ \mathfrak s^{\lambda}\), letter renaming and renamed schema,
  is listed both in the Greek and in the Fraktur table because its glyph
  carries a letter of each family.
\item A letter with no row whose scope contains the passage has no
  registered role there; the passage declares it, as
  \cref{def:p01-w02-p01-024-type-metavariables} explains for the
  lowercase Greek letters that a single statement or proof binds.
\end{enumerate}
\end{hbexample}

\section{Logical notation of Parts 1 and 2}
\label{sec:p01-w02b-p01-012-parts12}

Table~\ref{tab:p01-w02b-p01-012-parts12} lists every registry row selected
by item~4 of \cref{def:p01-w02b-p01-012-selection}, whatever its role:
56 rows of global scope, 395 with a
scope opening in Part~1, 987 with a scope opening in
Part~2, and the 3 rows whose scope label names Part~2 but is declared in
Part~3, 1441 rows in all.  Every connective, quantifier, turnstile, consequence or
satisfaction relation and metavariable that the registry records for these
parts is therefore a row of the table, printed with its recorded scope.
The last column is the recorded precedence: a numerical rank for an
object-language operator, \emph{meta} for a metalinguistic relation such as
a turnstile, \emph{object}, \emph{relation} or \emph{infix} for the
declared form of a symbol of the object language, and \emph{n/a} for a
symbol that is not an operator, such as a metavariable.

Among the rows are the classical and intuitionistic consequence relations
\(\Gamma\models_{\mathsf{CPL}}\varphi\), \(\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{IPL}}\varphi\)
and \(\Gamma\models_{\mathsf{IPL}}\varphi\) of the governing-logic section of
Part~1; derivability in a specified calculus, \(\Gamma\vdash_{\mathcal C}\varphi\),
with the scope \emph{sec:p01-w02-p01-019-consistency}; the binary
connectives \(\circ_k\) with code tag \(k\) of the decoding definition of
Part~1; Kleene's connectives \(\supset\) and \(\&\); the operation names
\(\mathrm{NAND}\) and \(\mathrm{NOR}\) of Post's classification in
Part~2; the exclusive-or abbreviation \(\oplus\) with the scope
\emph{sec:p02-w02-p02-035-decision-procedures}; the existence-committing
satisfaction and consequence relation \(\models_{\mathrm B}\) of the
historical contrast of Part~2; the constants and
derivability relations of Church's simple theory of types in Part~2; and
the family-member rows of the term and formula metavariables of Part~2.
A row with a narrower scope never overrides a global row outside that
scope, and the scope column is the only place where the extent of a role is
recorded.

\section{Readings of further Part 8 operators}
\label{sec:p01-w02b-p01-012-p8-readings}
\index{precedence!inquisitive connectives}\index{precedence!hybrid and temporal operators}\index{precedence!epistemic operators}\index{precedence!program constructors}

Forty-one operators of Part~8 are recorded in thirty registry rows, none
of which is listed in Table~\ref{tab:p01-w02-p01-024-ranks}.  Nineteen of
these rows record the rank that the convention below gives them and are
listed with it in Table~\ref{tab:p01-precedence}: the rows of items~1--3,
10, 11, 13--17 and~21 at rank~3 (fourteen rows), of item~4 at rank~3.5,
of items~5, 6 and~22 at rank~5, and of item~7 at rank~6.  The other eleven
rows, those of items~8, 9, 12, 18, 19, 20 and~23, have the precedence
entry \emph{n/a} and are not listed in Table~\ref{tab:p01-precedence};
item~12 nevertheless gives Scott's general monadic operator rank~3, and
item~18 gives rank~3 to the boxes formed with the two upgrades.
The thirty rows record the formula operators of the inquisitive,
neighborhood, hybrid, temporal and conditional languages (nine rows); the
indexed, universal and
difference modalities of the relational presentation, Scott's boxes and
quantifiers, and the knowledge, common-knowledge, announcement, belief and
upgrade operators of the epistemic sections (thirteen rows); the program
constructors, the test and the fixed-point binders of the dynamic and
fixed-point sections and the defined classical disjunction of the
inquisitive chapter (four rows); and the term-forming binders of Scott and
Montague (four rows).  Several of them share a letter with a ranked
operator of another scope: the ranked \(K_i\), \(C_G\), \(B_i\),
\([!\chi]A\), \(\langle a\rangle A\) and \([a]A\) of
Table~\ref{tab:p01-w02-p01-024-ranks} carry the scope
\emph{P8 conditionals-deontic-free}, and their ranks do not reach the
rows treated here, whose scopes are different.  The convention below gives
each of the forty-one operators the reading that the expressions printed in
its own section already presuppose, and its last four items say how the
remaining Part~8 rows with precedence \emph{n/a} combine, by kind.  Every
Part~8 registry row with that entry falls under one of the items, or names
a set, a structure, a calculus or language, a value, a number, a map or a
metavariable.
One further Part~8 formula operator is read here: the row of the binders
\((x)\) and \(\exists x\) of the \emph{Considerations} comparison
(\cref{sec:p08-n-b-009-02}) records rank~3, which item~28 below gives it,
and is listed with that rank in Table~\ref{tab:p01-precedence}.
The convention extends \cref{def:p01-w02-p01-024-fixity}, whose seven
clauses apply unchanged; in particular two different operators of the same
rank never compete, so parentheses are written between them.

\begin{hbconvention}{def:p01-w02b-p01-012-p8-readings}{Ranks, association and delimiter-fixed readings of further Part~8 operators}
Each item holds only inside the recorded scope named with it, and only
after the grammar of that scope has been fixed.  Ranks are those of
Table~\ref{tab:p01-precedence}: a smaller rank binds more tightly.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{Question mark} \(?\varphi\), scope \emph{P8 inquisitive-propositional}:
  rank~3, prefix.  It is the abbreviation
  \(?\varphi\defeq\varphi\mathbin{\vee\!\vee}\neg\varphi\) of
  \cref{def:p08-inq-language}; when the abbreviation is expanded inside a
  larger formula, the definiens is enclosed in parentheses.
\item \emph{Alternative modality} \(\langle\ ]\varphi\), scope
  \emph{P8 Pacuit neighborhood}: rank~3, prefix, like the box of the same
  language in \cref{def:p08-pn-language-model}.
\item \emph{Satisfaction operator} \(@_i\varphi\), scope
  \emph{sec:p08-bvb501-hybrid}: rank~3, prefix, the operand being the
  shortest formula after the nominal subscript, as for the rank-3 row of
  \(@_iA\) in Table~\ref{tab:p01-w02-p01-024-ranks}.
\item \emph{Strict until} \(A\mathbin{\mathsf U_s}B\), scope
  \emph{P8 linear temporal completeness}: rank~3.5, infix, nonassociative,
  the rank of \(A\mathbin{\mathsf U}B\) in Table~\ref{tab:p01-precedence}
  and its association in \cref{def:p01-w02-p01-026-precedence}.
\item \emph{Inquisitive disjunction} \(\varphi\mathbin{\vee\!\vee}\psi\),
  scope \emph{P8 inquisitive-propositional}: rank~5, infix, left
  associative.  The grammar of \cref{def:p08-inq-language} writes every
  occurrence in parentheses; the outermost pair is omitted, and an
  iterated inquisitive disjunction without inner parentheses is read from
  the left.
\item \emph{Tensor disjunction} \(\varphi\otimes\psi\), scope
  \emph{P8 inquisitive tensor extension}: rank~5, infix, left associative.
  This connective of \cref{def:p08-inq-tensor} is not the linear-logic
  tensor of rank~4; the two have different recorded scopes.
\item \emph{Conditional} \(\varphi>\psi\), scope
  \emph{sec:p08-bvb501-conditional}: rank~6, infix, nonassociative, as for
  the rank-6 conditional row of Table~\ref{tab:p01-w02-p01-024-ranks}.
\item \emph{Path until} \(U_{\exists}(\psi,\theta)\), scope
  \emph{sec:p08-bvb501-model-checking}: no rank.  The reading is fixed by
  the delimiters: the parentheses and the comma bound the two arguments
  completely, the first being the target condition and the second the
  condition on the earlier points of the path
  (\cref{def:p08-bvb501-until-check}), and the whole form is an
  indivisible operand for every other operator.
\item \emph{Interval until and since} \(U(\varphi,\psi)\) and
  \(S(\varphi,\psi)\), scope \emph{sec:p08-bvb501-temporal}: no rank; the
  reading is fixed by the delimiters as in item~8.  The first argument is
  the condition at the endpoint and the second the condition at every
  strictly intermediate point (\cref{def:p08-bvb501-until-since}).
\item \emph{Indexed diamond and box} \(\langle m\rangle\varphi\) and
  \([m]\varphi\), scope \emph{sec:p08-src-025-501}: rank~3, prefix; the
  modality name \(m\) between the delimiters is part of the operator.  The
  grammar of \cref{def:p08-bvb501-language} writes every binary
  combination in parentheses, so inside that grammar the reading is
  already fixed; the rank governs the expressions in which the text omits
  those parentheses.  The single-modality abbreviations \(\Diamond\) and
  \(\Box\) of the same definition are read in the same way.
\item \emph{Universal diamond and box} \(E\varphi\), \(A\varphi\) and
  \emph{difference diamond} \(D\varphi\), scope
  \emph{sec:p08-bvb501-universal}: rank~3, prefix
  (\cref{def:p08-bvb501-universal}).
\item \emph{Scott's general monadic operator} \(\boxed{\mathscr P}\Phi\),
  scope \emph{P8 Scott Advice}: rank~3, prefix
  (\cref{def:p08-sa-operators}).  The companion \(\mathscr P_i\) of the
  same registry row is not an operator: it names the family of index sets
  attached to the index \(i\), and it enters formulas only through the
  membership clause of that definition.
\item \emph{Scott's universal, relational and progressive boxes}
  \(\Box\Phi\), \(\boxed\rho\,\Phi\) and \(\boxed{\leftrightarrow}\Phi\),
  scope \emph{P8 Scott Advice}: rank~3, prefix; the relation \(\rho\) and
  the arrow inside the frame are part of the operator.
\item \emph{Scott's dotted actual quantifiers} \(\forall_{\cdot}x\,\Phi\)
  and \(\exists_{\cdot}x\,\Phi\), scope \emph{P8 Scott Advice}: rank~3,
  prefix, the rank of \(\forall x\) and \(\exists x\) in
  Table~\ref{tab:p01-precedence} (\cref{def:p08-sa-domains}).
\item \emph{Knowledge} \(K_i\psi\), scope \emph{p08 rationality-2-6}, and
  \emph{common knowledge} \(C\psi\), scope \emph{p08 epistemic update}:
  rank~3, prefix (\cref{def:p08-rat26-epistemic-update}).
\item \emph{Announcement box} \([!\phi]\psi\), scope
  \emph{p08 rationality-2-6}: the brackets delimit the announced formula
  \(\phi\) completely, and the box so formed is a rank-3 prefix operator
  on the formula that follows it.
\item \emph{Belief} \(B\psi\) and \emph{conditional belief}
  \(B^\chi\psi\), scope \emph{p08 soft information}: rank~3, prefix
  (\cref{def:p08-rat26-soft-update}); the superscript delimits the
  condition \(\chi\) completely.
\item \emph{Conservative and radical upgrade} \(\uparrow\phi\) and
  \(\Uparrow\phi\), scope \emph{p08 soft information}: these form update
  actions, not formulas.  Inside the box brackets \([\uparrow\phi]\) and
  \([\Uparrow\phi]\) the operand of the arrow is the whole formula up to
  the closing bracket, and the box so formed is a rank-3 prefix operator,
  as in item~16.
\item \emph{Test} \(\chi?\), scope \emph{sec:p08-bvb501-pdl}: postfix; it
  forms a program from a formula (\cref{def:p08-bvb501-pdl}).  Its operand
  is the longest formula that ends immediately before the sign \(?\).  Thus in \((\neg p?;a)\) it is
  \(\neg p\), and in \(\langle a\rangle p?\) it is \(\langle a\rangle p\).
\item \emph{Program constructors} \(\pi^*\), \(\pi;\rho\) and
  \(\pi\cup\rho\), scope \emph{sec:p08-bvb501-pdl}: these combine
  programs, and they are ranked on a separate program scale, because a
  program position and a formula position are never adjacent to the same
  operand.  Iteration \({}^*\) is postfix with program rank~1 and takes the
  immediately preceding atomic program or parenthesized program;
  composition \(;\) has program rank~2 and is left associative; choice
  \(\cup\) has program rank~3 and is left associative.  A program inside a
  diamond \(\langle\pi\rangle\) or a box \([\pi]\) is delimited by its
  brackets, and the diamond or box so formed is a rank-3 prefix operator.
\item \emph{Fixed-point binders} \(\mu p.\chi\) and \(\nu p.\chi\), scope
  \emph{sec:p08-bvb501-mu}: \cref{def:p08-bvb501-mu} states no rule for
  the extent of the body after the dot, so by clause~5 of
  \cref{def:p01-w02-p01-024-fixity} each binder is a rank-3 prefix binder
  and clause~4 applies: the body is the shortest formula after the dot.
  A substitution \([\psi/p]\) written directly after a formula letter
  applies to that letter alone.
\item \emph{Defined classical disjunction} \(\varphi\lor\psi\), scope
  \emph{P8 inquisitive-propositional}: rank~5, infix, left associative,
  the rank of \(\lor\) in Table~\ref{tab:p01-precedence} and its
  association in \cref{def:p01-w02-p01-026-precedence}.
  When its definition by negation and conjunction is expanded inside a
  larger formula, the definiens is enclosed in parentheses; next to an
  inquisitive disjunction, which shares rank~5, parentheses are written.
\item \emph{Term-forming binders}: Scott's description binders
  \(\mathsf I x\,\Phi\) and \(\mathsf I_{\cdot}x\,\Phi\) and his general
  binder \(\$x[\tau(x),\Phi(x)]\), scope \emph{P8 Scott Advice}
  (\cref{def:p08-sa-concepts,def:p08-sa-binding}); Montague's predicate
  description \(\mathsf T G\phi\) and predicate names
  \(\widehat{\bar u}\phi\) and \(\widehat\phi\), scope
  \emph{P8 Montague Synthese 1970 intensional logic}
  (\cref{def:p08-montague-intensional-language,def:p08-montague-predicate-names}).
  Each forms a term, not a formula, and has no rank.  The arguments of
  \(\$\) are delimited by its brackets and its comma.  In \(\widehat\phi\)
  the body is the formula under the accent.  For the other binders the
  body is the shortest formula after the bound variable, or after the
  variable list (prefix operators at the start of the body nest by
  clause~4 of \cref{def:p01-w02-p01-024-fixity}), and the term so formed is one argument of the predicate, equality or argument place that
  contains it.
\end{enumerate}
The remaining Part~8 rows with precedence \emph{n/a} are read by kind.
\begin{enumerate}\setcounter{enumi}{23}
\item \emph{Application and bracket forms.}  A predicate symbol, a named
  abbreviation or a syntactic map applied to a delimited argument list,
  such as \(\operatorname{One}(B_1,\ldots,B_k)\),
  \(\operatorname{Alt}(\mathcal A)\), \(\operatorname{Rel}(\chi,p)\),
  \(\operatorname{ST}_x(\varphi)\) or \(T_\pi(\eta)\), is read by the
  rank-1 application of \cref{def:p01-w02-p01-026-precedence}: the
  delimited arguments belong to the displayed symbol.  Bracket notations
  for truth sets, support propositions, classes and updated valuations or assignments, such as
  \([\varphi]_M\), \(|\varphi|_M\), \([\![\sigma]\!]\), \([w]_\Sigma\),
  \(V[p\leftarrow U]\) and \(g[x\mapsto d]\), delimit their argument
  completely.  An accent or a superscript that denotes a map on formulas
  or sets, such as \(\widehat\alpha\), \(\overline\alpha\),
  \(\widetilde K\), \(K^p\) or \(\varphi^{\mathrm{cl}}\), applies to the
  single letter or the bracketed expression that carries it.  In each case
  the whole form is one operand.
\item \emph{Relations.}  A relation symbol between worlds, states, points
  or values, such as \(R_m\), \(R_\pi\), \(\sim\), \(\sim_\Sigma\),
  \(\preceq\) or \(\to^*\), is a rank-2 atomic relation and is
  nonassociative.
\item \emph{Operations of the semantics and on logics.}  Operations on the
  carriers of algebras and chains, on worlds, on sets of worlds or states,
  and on logics (the Heyting and Boolean operations, the conditional
  operation \(\Rightarrow\) of a G\"odel chain, the fusion \(\otimes\) of two normal
  modal logics, the Brouwerian \(+\), \(\cdot\), \(\mathbin{\dot-}\)
  and \(\mathord\sim\), the closure and interior operators, the normal
  diamond \(m\) of a Boolean algebra, \(\operatorname{pre}_a\),
  \(\operatorname{box}_a\), \(l(X)\), \(\operatorname{int}\),
  \(\operatorname{cl}\), \(T^\downarrow\), \({\downarrow}S\), \({\uparrow}w\)
  and the Routley star) are not formula operators and receive no rank; an
  expression that combines two different ones without parentheses is not
  licensed by this convention.
\item \emph{Consequence and assertion signs.}  The support, consequence,
  derivability and equivalence signs of Part~8 recorded without a rank
  (\(\models_s\), \(\models_M\), \(\equiv_{\mathrm{InqB}}\),
  \(\vdash_{\mathrm{InqB}}\), \(\vdash_L\), \(\vdash_L^{\rm fin}\),
  \(\models_\omega\), the derivability signs of the minimal and
  intermediate presentations, \(\models_{-,X}\),
  \(\vdash^{\mathrm{tr}}_{-,X}\) and \(\dashv\vdash^{\mathrm{tr}}_{-,X}\)),
  and the assertion sign \(\sim\) of \(K\vdash_P\alpha\sim\beta\), are
  read like the metalinguistic signs with the precedence descriptor
  \texttt{meta} in the last item of \cref{def:p01-w02-p01-026-precedence}:
  each side is a whole formula or a whole set of formulas.  An assertion
  \(\alpha\sim\beta\) is a pair of formulas and is never an operand of a
  connective (\cref{def:p08-klm-assertions}).
\end{enumerate}
The local-domain binders of the \emph{Considerations} comparison are read
as follows.
\begin{enumerate}\setcounter{enumi}{27}
\item \emph{Local-domain binders} \((x)A\) and \(\exists x\,A\), the
  latter also printed \((\exists x)A\), scope \emph{P8 Considerations
  supporting comparison} (\cref{def:p08-kc-model}): rank~3, prefix, the
  rank of \(\forall x\) and \(\exists x\) in
  Table~\ref{tab:p01-precedence}.  \Cref{def:p08-kc-model} lists the
  primitive symbols of the language (conjunction, negation, \(\Box\) and
  the binder) and treats implication and the other Boolean operations as
  abbreviations, but it states no rule for writing or omitting
  parentheses.  Inside this scope \(\neg\), \(\Box\) and \(\Diamond\) have
  rank~3, \(\land\) rank~4, \(\lor\) rank~5, \(\to\) rank~6 and
  \(\leftrightarrow\) rank~7, as in Table~\ref{tab:p01-precedence}, and by
  clause~4 of \cref{def:p01-w02-p01-024-fixity} the binder takes the
  shortest formula after its variable; prefix operators at the start of
  that formula nest.  The parentheses of \((x)\) and of \((\exists x)\)
  enclose the bound variable only and belong to the binder; they never
  delimit its body.  A parenthesized variable written directly after a
  formula letter or a predicate letter, as in \(A(x)\), \(P(x)\) or
  \(E(y)\), is the argument list of that letter (rank~1), not a binder, so
  \((x)A(x)\) is the binder \((x)\) applied to the formula \(A(x)\).  The
  registry records \(\exists x\) as the Boolean dual of the binder,
  \(\exists x\,A\) standing for \(\neg(x)\neg A\); when that definiens is
  expanded inside a larger formula it is enclosed in parentheses.
\end{enumerate}
The Part~8 rows recorded with the precedence \emph{object} or
\emph{relation} are not formula operators.  Each names a set, a structure,
a system or schema, an assignment, a map on formulas, an operation of the
semantics or a relation, and where it combines with other symbols it is
read by the item of its kind among items~24--27.
\end{hbconvention}

Items~3 and~10 hold in the body section of the relational presentation.
Its annex proofs, the chapter that defines the temporal and epistemic
operators of Part~8, the chapter of the linear calculi and the box of the
inquisitive chapter receive the corresponding ranks in
\cref{def:p01-w02c-p01-017-p8-chapters}.

The ranks agree with the source of the inquisitive chapter and with the
way the Part~8 text writes these operators.  Ciardelli's grammar puts every
binary connective in parentheses, defines the question mark as the
abbreviation \(\varphi\mathbin{\vee\!\vee}\neg\varphi\)
\cite[Definitions~3.2.1--3.2.2, p.~60]{Ciardelli2023InquisitivePropositional},
and drops brackets only where no confusion arises
\cite[\S3.1, p.~57]{Ciardelli2023InquisitivePropositional}.  For the
first-order language the same book fixes priorities: quantifiers and
negation bind most tightly, conjunctions and disjunctions, inquisitive
disjunction among them, come next, and implication binds least
\cite[\S5.1, pp.~125--126]{Ciardelli2023InquisitivePropositional}.
Rank~5 for inquisitive disjunction respects each of these comparisons.  The
inquisitive section writes \(?p\land?q\), \(?p\to?q\), \(\neg?p\) and
\(?\neg p\) for \((?p)\land(?q)\), \((?p)\to(?q)\), \(\neg(?p)\) and
\(?(\neg p)\), and puts parentheses in \(?(p\land q)\), which a rank-3
prefix operator needs.  It writes
\((a_1\lor a_2)\mathbin{\vee\!\vee}(a_3\lor a_4)\mathbin{\vee\!\vee}(a_5\lor a_6)\)
in \cref{def:p08-inq-die-proof}, with parentheses between the classical
and the inquisitive disjunction, which share rank~5.  The hybrid section
writes \(@_ij\to@_ji\) and \(@_j\Diamond i\lor@_ji\lor@_i\Diamond j\), which
read \((@_ij)\to(@_ji)\) and
\(((@_j(\Diamond i))\lor(@_ji))\lor(@_i(\Diamond j))\).

The same holds for the modal and epistemic operators.  In the relational
presentation the printed duality
\([m]\varphi\leftrightarrow\neg\langle m\rangle\neg\varphi\) reads
\(([m]\varphi)\leftrightarrow(\neg(\langle m\rangle(\neg\varphi)))\).  The
local and global constraint \(\varphi\land A\psi\) of
\cref{thm:p08-bvb501-universal-expressivity} reads
\(\varphi\land(A\psi)\), the predecessor condition \(p\to E\Diamond p\)
reads \(p\to(E(\Diamond p))\), the well-foundedness formula
\(A(\Box p\to p)\to p\) of \cref{thm:p08-bvb501-universal-well-founded}
reads \((A(\Box p\to p))\to p\), and
\(E\varphi\leftrightarrow(\varphi\lor D\varphi)\) of
\cref{thm:p08-bvb501-difference} reads
\((E\varphi)\leftrightarrow(\varphi\lor(D\varphi))\).  Scott's section writes
\(\Box\forall x\Phi(x)\leftrightarrow\forall x\Box\Phi(x)\) in
\cref{thm:p08-sa-universal-quantifiers}, read
\((\Box(\forall x\,\Phi(x)))\leftrightarrow(\forall x\,(\Box\Phi(x)))\),
and puts parentheses in \(\Box(\Phi\leftrightarrow\Psi)\) and
\(\Box(\tau=\sigma)\), which a rank-3 box needs.  The epistemic sections
write \([!\phi]C\phi\), \(\neg K\neg\phi\), \(\neg B^\chi q\),
\([\uparrow\phi]B\phi\) and \([\Uparrow\phi]B\phi\) in
\cref{thm:p08-rat26-hard-update,thm:p08-rat26-soft-update}, read
\([!\phi](C\phi)\), \(\neg(K(\neg\phi))\), \(\neg(B^\chi q)\),
\([\uparrow\phi](B\phi)\) and \([\Uparrow\phi](B\phi)\).  The program
encodings displayed after \cref{def:p08-bvb501-pdl} write parentheses
wherever \(;\) meets \(\cup\) or \({}^*\) applies to a compound program, as
in \((p?;a)\cup(\neg p?;b)\) and \((p?;a)^*;\neg p?\), and the
fixed-point section writes \(\mu p.(\chi\lor\langle a\rangle p)\) and
\(\nu p.(\chi\land[a]p)\) with the parentheses that a rank-3 binder needs
for a compound body.

\begin{hblemma}{thm:p01-w02b-p01-012-iterated-support}{proved=annex:p01-w02b-p01-012-iterated-support}{Bracketing does not affect support of iterated inquisitive disjunctions}
Let \(M=(W,V)\) be a propositional information model, let \(s\subseteq W\),
let \(n\geq1\), and let \(\varphi_1,\ldots,\varphi_n\) be formulas of
\(\mathsf{InqB}^{\otimes}\).  For every bracketing \(\delta\) of
\(\varphi_1\mathbin{\vee\!\vee}\cdots\mathbin{\vee\!\vee}\varphi_n\) and
every bracketing \(\tau\) of
\(\varphi_1\otimes\cdots\otimes\varphi_n\), each with the operands in
this order,
\begin{enumerate}
\item \(M,s\models\delta\) if and only if \(M,s\models\varphi_i\) for some
  \(i\leq n\);
\item \(M,s\models\tau\) if and only if \(s=s_1\cup\cdots\cup s_n\) for
  some \(s_1,\ldots,s_n\subseteq W\) with \(M,s_i\models\varphi_i\) for
  every \(i\leq n\).
\end{enumerate}
In particular any two bracketings of either iterated disjunction are
supported by the same states of every model, so the left association of
items~5 and~6 of \cref{def:p01-w02b-p01-012-p8-readings} selects one
formula from a class of support-equivalent formulas.
\end{hblemma}

\begin{hblemma}{thm:p01-w02b-p01-012-strict-reading}{proved=annex:p01-w02b-p01-012-strict-reading}{Readings of the strict-until bridge}
Under \cref{def:p01-w02-p01-026-precedence,def:p01-w02-p01-024-fixity},
Table~\ref{tab:p01-precedence} and item~4 of
\cref{def:p01-w02b-p01-012-p8-readings}:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \(A\mathbin{\mathsf U_s}B\leftrightarrow\mathsf X(A\mathbin{\mathsf U}B)\)
  has exactly the reading
  \((A\mathbin{\mathsf U_s}B)\leftrightarrow(\mathsf X(A\mathbin{\mathsf U}B))\);
\item \(A\mathbin{\mathsf U}B\leftrightarrow B\lor(A\land(A\mathbin{\mathsf U_s}B))\)
  and \(A\mathbin{\mathsf U}B\leftrightarrow B\lor(A\land A\mathbin{\mathsf U_s}B)\)
  both have exactly the reading
  \((A\mathbin{\mathsf U}B)\leftrightarrow\bigl(B\lor(A\land(A\mathbin{\mathsf U_s}B))\bigr)\);
\item \(A\mathbin{\mathsf U}B\mathbin{\mathsf U_s}C\),
  \(A\mathbin{\mathsf U_s}B\mathbin{\mathsf U}C\) and
  \(A\mathbin{\mathsf U_s}B\mathbin{\mathsf U_s}C\) have no reading.
\end{enumerate}
So the two biconditionals displayed in \cref{thm:p08-ltl-strict-bridge}
have the readings that its statement compares.
\end{hblemma}

\begin{hblemma}{thm:p01-w02b-p01-012-epistemic-reading}{proved=annex:p01-w02b-p01-012-epistemic-reading}{Readings of the announcement law and the fixed-point identity}
Under \cref{def:p01-w02-p01-026-precedence,def:p01-w02-p01-024-fixity},
Table~\ref{tab:p01-precedence} and items~15, 16 and~21 of
\cref{def:p01-w02b-p01-012-p8-readings}:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \([!\phi]K_i\psi\leftrightarrow(\phi\to K_i[!\phi]\psi)\) has exactly
  the reading
  \(\bigl([!\phi](K_i\psi)\bigr)\leftrightarrow\bigl(\phi\to(K_i([!\phi]\psi))\bigr)\);
\item \(\neg\mu p.\neg\chi[\neg p/p]\) has exactly the reading
  \(\neg\bigl(\mu p.\bigl(\neg(\chi[\neg p/p])\bigr)\bigr)\).
\end{enumerate}
So the reduction law of \cref{thm:p08-rat26-hard-update} compares the
announcement box applied to \(K_i\psi\) with an implication whose
consequent is \(K_i\) applied to the box, and the identity
\(\nu p.\chi=\neg\mu p.\neg\chi[\neg p/p]\) of \cref{def:p08-bvb501-mu}
negates the whole least fixed point.
\end{hblemma}

\begin{hblemma}{thm:p01-w02b-p01-012-program-bracketing}{proved=annex:p01-w02b-p01-012-program-bracketing}{Bracketing does not affect the relation of an iterated composition or choice}
Let \(M\) be a regular model in the sense of \cref{def:p08-bvb501-pdl}, let
\(n\geq1\), and let \(\pi_1,\ldots,\pi_n\) be programs.  For every
bracketing \(\gamma\) of \(\pi_1;\cdots;\pi_n\) and every bracketing
\(\kappa\) of \(\pi_1\cup\cdots\cup\pi_n\), each with the operands in this
order,
\begin{enumerate}
\item \((w,v)\in R_\gamma\) if and only if there are states
  \(u_0,\ldots,u_n\) with \(u_0=w\), \(u_n=v\) and
  \((u_{i-1},u_i)\in R_{\pi_i}\) for every \(i\leq n\);
\item \(R_\kappa=R_{\pi_1}\cup\cdots\cup R_{\pi_n}\).
\end{enumerate}
Hence any two bracketings of either iterated program have the same
transition relation, and \(\langle\gamma\rangle\chi\), \([\gamma]\chi\),
\(\langle\kappa\rangle\chi\) and \([\kappa]\chi\) are true at the same
states for every bracketing, so the left association of item~20 of
\cref{def:p01-w02b-p01-012-p8-readings} selects one program from a class
of programs with one relation.
\end{hblemma}

\begin{hblemma}{thm:p01-w02b-p01-012-considerations-reading}{proved=annex:p01-w02b-p01-012-considerations-reading}{Readings of the binder formulas of the Considerations comparison}
Under \cref{def:p01-w02-p01-026-precedence,def:p01-w02-p01-024-fixity},
Table~\ref{tab:p01-precedence} and item~28 of
\cref{def:p01-w02b-p01-012-p8-readings}:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \(A\to(x)A\) has exactly the reading \(A\to((x)A)\);
\item \((x)(A\to B)\to((x)A\to(x)B)\) has exactly the reading
  \(\bigl((x)(A\to B)\bigr)\to\bigl(((x)A)\to((x)B)\bigr)\);
\item \((x)A(x)\to A(y)\) has exactly the reading
  \(((x)A(x))\to A(y)\), and \((y)((x)A(x)\to A(y))\) has exactly the
  reading \((y)\bigl(((x)A(x))\to A(y)\bigr)\);
\item \((x)\Box A(x)\to\Box(x)A(x)\) has exactly the reading
  \(\bigl((x)(\Box A(x))\bigr)\to\bigl(\Box((x)A(x))\bigr)\), and
  \(\Box(x)A(x)\to(x)\Box A(x)\) has exactly the reading
  \(\bigl(\Box((x)A(x))\bigr)\to\bigl((x)(\Box A(x))\bigr)\);
\item \(((x)A(x)\land E(y))\to A(y)\) has exactly the reading
  \(\bigl(((x)A(x))\land E(y)\bigr)\to A(y)\);
\item the reading of item~3 differs from the wide-scope formula
  \((x)(A(x)\to A(y))\): for a unary predicate letter \(P\), a source
  model structure with \(\psi(G)=\{a,b\}\) for distinct individuals
  \(a,b\) and \(\varphi(P,G)=\{a\}\), and an assignment \(s\) with
  \(s(y)=b\), the formula \(((x)P(x))\to P(y)\) is true at \(G\) and
  \((x)(P(x)\to P(y))\) is false at \(G\) under \(s\).
\end{enumerate}
So the closure schemas of \cref{def:p08-kc-closures}, the instantiation
assertion of \cref{thm:p08-kc-necessitation-boundary}, the two Barcan
directions of \cref{def:p08-kc-barcan} and the existence axiom of
\cref{def:p08-kc-existence} are implications whose binders govern only the
displayed formula after them; in particular \(\mathrm{BF}\) compares
\((x)\Box A(x)\) with \(\Box(x)A(x)\), the two formulas whose truth values
the countermodels of \cref{thm:p08-kc-s5-countermodels} separate.
\end{hblemma}

\begin{hbexample}{def:p01-w02b-p01-012-p8-examples}{Reading the Part~8 operators}
\begin{enumerate}
\item In \(\mathsf{InqB}\), \(p\land q\mathbin{\vee\!\vee}r\) is
  \((p\land q)\mathbin{\vee\!\vee}r\), because conjunction (rank~4) binds
  more tightly than inquisitive disjunction (rank~5); the formula
  \(p\land(q\mathbin{\vee\!\vee}r)\) of the resolution calculations in the
  inquisitive section is a different formula and keeps its parentheses.
\item \(?m\to a\mathbin{\vee\!\vee}b\) is
  \((?m)\to(a\mathbin{\vee\!\vee}b)\); the parentheses that the
  inquisitive section prints around \(a\mathbin{\vee\!\vee}b\) are
  therefore redundant but harmless.
\item \(p\lor q\mathbin{\vee\!\vee}r\) and
  \(p\mathbin{\vee\!\vee}q\otimes r\) have no reading: the operators share
  rank~5 and are different.
\item \(K\otimes K\otimes K\) is \((K\otimes K)\otimes K\); by
  \cref{thm:p01-w02b-p01-012-iterated-support} it is supported exactly by
  the unions of three states supporting \(K\).
\item \(U(p,q)\land r\) is \((U(p,q))\land r\), and
  \(\neg U_{\exists}(\psi,\theta)\) is \(\neg(U_{\exists}(\psi,\theta))\).
\item \(\varphi>\psi>\chi\) and \(\varphi>\psi\to\chi\) have no reading;
  the first is excluded by the missing association, the second because
  \(>\) and \(\to\) share rank~6.
\item \([m]p\land\langle m\rangle q\to D\topv\) is
  \(\bigl(([m]p)\land(\langle m\rangle q)\bigr)\to(D\topv)\), and
  \(\neg E\neg\varphi\) is \(\neg(E(\neg\varphi))\).
\item \(K_iK_jp\to p\) is \((K_i(K_jp))\to p\), by clause~4 of
  \cref{def:p01-w02-p01-024-fixity} for the two prefix operators and
  clause~1 for the operand \(K_jp\); \(\neg C\neg p\) is
  \(\neg(C(\neg p))\); and \(B^\chi q\lor B\neg p\) is
  \((B^\chi q)\lor(B(\neg p))\).
\item The PDL program \(a;(\neg p?;a)^*;p?\) is
  \(\bigl(a;((\neg p)?;a)^*\bigr);(p?)\): the test takes the whole
  formula \(\neg p\) after the opening parenthesis, iteration takes the
  parenthesized program, and composition associates to the left.  By
  \cref{thm:p01-w02b-p01-012-program-bracketing} the right-associated
  bracketing has the same relation.  The program \(a;b\cup c\) is
  \((a;b)\cup c\).
\item \(\nu p.\langle a\rangle p\land q\) is
  \((\nu p.\langle a\rangle p)\land q\); the source's
  \(\nu p.(\chi\land[a]p)\) keeps its parentheses because the body is
  compound.
\item Scott's \(\mathsf I x\Phi(x)=\mathsf I x(x\ne x)\) is the equation
  \((\mathsf I x\,\Phi(x))=(\mathsf I x\,(x\ne x))\) between two
  terms, and his example \(\mathsf I y\forall x\Box(\Phi(x)\to y=\tau(x))\)
  in \cref{def:p08-sa-binding} binds \(y\) in the body
  \(\forall x\,(\Box(\Phi(x)\to y=\tau(x)))\).
\item In the \emph{Considerations} comparison, \(\Box(x)E(x)\) of
  \cref{thm:p08-kc-existence} is \(\Box((x)E(x))\) and \((x)\Box E(x)\) is
  \((x)(\Box E(x))\).  The instantiation assertion
  \((x)P(x)\to P(y)\) is \(((x)P(x))\to P(y)\).  Take
  \(\psi(G)=\{a,b\}\), \(\varphi(P,G)=\{a\}\) and \(s(y)=b\): the
  antecedent \((x)P(x)\) is false at \(G\), because \(b\in\psi(G)\) and
  \(b\notin\varphi(P,G)\), so the implication is true.  Reading the binder
  as taking the whole implication gives \((x)(P(x)\to P(y))\), which is
  false at \(G\), because the value \(a\) of \(x\) makes \(P(x)\) true and
  \(P(y)\) false (\cref{thm:p01-w02b-p01-012-considerations-reading}).
\end{enumerate}
\end{hbexample}

\begin{hbwarning}{def:p01-w02b-p01-012-p8-limits}{Limits of the Part~8 readings}
\begin{enumerate}
\item Expanding \(?\varphi\) without the parentheses of item~1 changes the
  formula.  The abbreviation \(?p\land q\) stands for
  \((p\mathbin{\vee\!\vee}\neg p)\land q\), whereas
  \(p\mathbin{\vee\!\vee}\neg p\land q\) reads
  \(p\mathbin{\vee\!\vee}((\neg p)\land q)\).  At a one-world state where
  \(p\) is true and \(q\) false, the second is supported, because the state
  supports \(p\), and the first is not, because the state does not support
  \(q\).
\item The bracketed operators \(U(\varphi,\psi)\) and \(S(\varphi,\psi)\)
  of \cref{def:p08-bvb501-until-since} put the endpoint condition first,
  whereas in the infix \(A\mathbin{\mathsf U}B\) of
  Table~\ref{tab:p01-precedence} the endpoint condition \(B\) is the right
  operand; Kamp's prefix connectives \(U\,A\,B\), \(S\,A\,B\) at rank~3
  in Table~\ref{tab:p01-w02-p01-024-ranks} are a third notation.  The
  three are recorded in different scopes and are not interchangeable by
  copying operands.
\item \(U_{\exists}(\psi,\theta)\) asks for one finite path; the interval
  clause that the same section prints quantifies over all intermediate
  points and differs from it on graphs with branching and merging
  (\cref{thm:p08-bvb501-until-check}).  A shared letter \(U\) does not make
  the two operators one.
\item Ciardelli's priority convention puts conjunction on the same level as
  the disjunctions \cite[\S5.1, pp.~125--126]{Ciardelli2023InquisitivePropositional},
  whereas the handbook ranks conjunction at~4 and the disjunctions at~5.
  The two conventions therefore differ on a string such as
  \(p\land q\mathbin{\vee\!\vee}r\): the handbook reads it as
  \((p\land q)\mathbin{\vee\!\vee}r\), and the source's statement gives no
  priority between the two connectives.  A formula transcribed from the
  source with conjunction next to a disjunction keeps its parentheses.
\item \cref{thm:p01-w02b-p01-012-iterated-support,thm:p01-w02b-p01-012-program-bracketing}
  concern support and transition relations only.  Two bracketings of an
  iterated disjunction or program remain different expressions, so a count
  of subformulas or subprograms, a translation clause applied step by step
  (such as \(T_{\pi;\rho}(\eta)=T_\pi(T_\rho(\eta))\) of
  \cref{thm:p08-bvb501-mu-constructions}), or a derivation step that copies
  an expression verbatim depends on the bracketing that the convention
  selects.
\item One letter carries several roles across Part~8, and a rank given here
  holds only for the row named.  The common-knowledge operator \(C\) of
  \emph{p08 epistemic update} has no group index, unlike the ranked
  \(C_G\) of \emph{P8 conditionals-deontic-free}; \(C\) is also the injury
  cost of the Hawk--Dove rows and the closure operator of the closure
  algebra rows.  The universal modality \(E\) shares its letter with the
  bisimulation relation \(E\) of \emph{sec:p08-src-025-501} and with the
  group operator \(E_G\); the difference diamond \(D\) shares its letter
  with the distributed-knowledge operator \(D_G\), with a set of designated
  values and with an outer domain.  The upgrade arrow \({\uparrow}\phi\)
  of \emph{p08 soft information} is not the principal upset
  \({\uparrow}w\) of the topological rows.  Scott's box of
  \emph{P8 Scott Advice} is the universal box of his framework, true at an
  index only when its argument holds at every index; it is not the box of
  an arbitrary Kripke frame of the ranked row \(\Box,\Diamond\).
\item The program ranks of item~20 are a separate scale and are never
  compared with formula ranks: a test \(\chi?\) contains a formula, read by
  the formula ranks, inside a program.  The choice that composition binds
  more tightly than choice is a convention of this chapter; the encodings
  displayed after \cref{def:p08-bvb501-pdl} print parentheses where the two
  meet, so none of them depends on it.
\item Item~23 gives no rank to a term-forming binder.  A string in which
  such a binder is followed by a formula and then by a connective, such as
  \(\mathsf I x\,\Phi(x)\land\Psi\), has no reading as a formula: the
  binder takes \(\Phi(x)\), and the resulting term cannot be an operand
  of \(\land\).
\item The rank of item~28 holds only in the scope \emph{P8 Considerations
  supporting comparison}.  The binder \((x)\) ranges over the local domain
  \(\psi(H)\) of the world of evaluation; Fitting's primitive \(\forall\)
  and his relativized \(\forall^E\) of \cref{def:p08-qml-model} are other
  rows with other scopes, and the translation of
  \cref{thm:p08-kc-fitting-bridge} replaces \((x)A\) by
  \((\forall^Ex)t(A)\) as a whole formula, so a translated formula keeps
  the bracketing that item~28 gives to the source formula.  The
  parenthesized notation also has a hazard of its own: in a string such as
  \((x)A\) the pair \((x)\) is a binder, whereas in \(A(x)\) it is an
  argument list, and only the position directly after a letter tells the
  two apart.
\end{enumerate}
\end{hbwarning}

\section{Relationships}
\label{sec:p01-w02b-p01-012-relationships}

The tables of this chapter extend the letter-family extracts of
\cref{sec:p01-w02-p01-024-letters}, which were drawn from an earlier state
of the registry, to every row of the registry state named in the inputs
paragraph above; where the two disagree, the tables here are the current
extract.  The compact summary of the letter families in
Table~\ref{tab:p01-letter-families} and the classical sub-register of
\cref{sec:p01-symbol-registry} remain the starting points for lookup.
The convention \cref{def:p01-w02b-p01-012-p8-readings} sets
readings for the forty-one operators of thirty registry rows and for the
local-domain binders of the \emph{Considerations} comparison beside the
ranked operators of
Table~\ref{tab:p01-w02-p01-024-ranks}, with a separate program scale for
the constructors of propositional dynamic logic; the general
clauses of \cref{def:p01-w02-p01-024-fixity}, including the boundary that
a named grammar fixes its own readings, govern them.  The same program
scale, the autoepistemic operator of Part~9 and the denial of the
selection-task section of Part~19 are treated in
\cref{def:p01-w02c-p01-017-readings}.  The difference
between the atomic diagram and the positive diagram, which concerns the
model-theory sub-register, is treated in
\cref{thm:p01-w02-p01-024-positive-diagram}.

\emph{Sources.}  The extracts reproduce the rows of
\texttt{registry/symbols.csv}.  The readings of the Part~8 operators rest
on the handbook definitions cited with each item: the support semantics
and the tensor extension of the inquisitive chapter
(\cref{def:p08-inq-language,def:p08-inq-support,def:p08-inq-tensor}), the
strict-until bridge (\cref{thm:p08-ltl-strict-bridge}), the neighborhood
language (\cref{def:p08-pn-language-model}), the hybrid, temporal,
model-checking and conditional definitions
(\cref{def:p08-bvb501-hybrid,def:p08-bvb501-until-since,def:p08-bvb501-until-check,def:p08-bvb501-conditional}),
the relational language, the universal and difference modalities, the
dynamic and fixed-point definitions
(\cref{def:p08-bvb501-language,def:p08-bvb501-universal,def:p08-bvb501-pdl,def:p08-bvb501-mu}),
Scott's and Montague's operation and binder definitions
(\cref{def:p08-sa-domains,def:p08-sa-concepts,def:p08-sa-operators,def:p08-sa-binding,def:p08-montague-intensional-language,def:p08-montague-predicate-names}),
the hard and soft information definitions
(\cref{def:p08-rat26-epistemic-update,def:p08-rat26-soft-update}) and the
conditional assertions of \cref{def:p08-klm-assertions}.
The bracket and priority conventions of the inquisitive source are those of
\cite[\S3.1, p.~57; Definitions~3.2.1--3.2.2, p.~60; \S5.1, pp.~125--126]{Ciardelli2023InquisitivePropositional}.
